ON TRIAL REVIEW
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& AM. of California:
There have been referred to your Committee on Trial Review, since the
1991 Annual Communication of Grand Lodge. eighteen (18) trials. of which
three (3) are applications for Restoration.
Your Comminee has reviewed each of the Trial Records numbered
2330,2332.2333.2334.2335, 2336.2338.2339.2340,2342.2343and2344
and finds that in each case there was substantial compliance with the
procedural requirements of our Constitution and Ordinances. and the result
is supported by the evidence in the records of each case and that there are
no miscarriages of justice.

393

Resolution ADOPTED.
Trial Record No. 2173
In Trial Record No. 2173. a former member of Malta Lodge No. 588 (now
known as Malta El Sereno No. 588i was charged with a violation of California
Penal Code Section 647a. in that he molested his step-daughter. a child
under the age of 18 years. He entered a plea of "Not Guilty," and a jury trial
was held on May 18, 1978 in which he was found "Guilty.' One of the
conditions of his sentence was that he register pursuant to California Penal
Code Section 290, as a sex offender.
After the conviction and sentence. which was affirmed on appeal,

Your committee therefore submits the following Resolution:
RESOLVED. that in each of the above numbered Trial Records. the
judgment and penalty imposed be and are ratified. affirmed and
approved.
Resolution ADOPTED.
Trial Record No. 2136
The Petitioner. a former member of Bell Lodge No. 483. was expelled
by Grand Lodge in 1979 for unmasonic conduct arising from a conviction
of receiving stolen property.
The Petitioner applied for restoration in 1980 and it was denied by Grand
Lodge. The basis for the denial was the failure of the Petitioner's former
lodge to recommend restoration, and a failure on his part to show any
evidence of rehabilitation.
The Petitioner has again applied for restoration. His lodge has again
voted against his restoration and his petition again fails to show evidence of
remorse or rehabilitation.
We therefore submit the following Resolution:

charges of unmasonic conduct wern filed on October 21. 1978. He was
found guilty of unmasonic conduct and the penalty was fixed at expulsion.
This was affirmed by Grand Lodge in October. 1982.
He has applied for restoration on four previous occasions· September
23. 1987. June S. 1989. May 14. 1990 and again in July, 1991: and these four
applications wer~ denied by Grand Lodge.
He again seeks restoration and has ottered letters of personal reference
and recommendations. However. this year he has presented additional
independent evidence of remorse and has had his criminal case dismissed
pursuant to the California Penal Code. Because of these factors, we believe
that the Petitioner was entitled to request that his lodge vote upon his
restoration: this they did at the July Stated Meeting: and voted unanimously
in favor of restoration.
Your Comminee on Trial Review submits the following Resolution
RESOLVED. that the Petition for Restoration in Trial Record No.
2173 be approved.
Resolution ADOPTED.

Trial Record No. 2296

The Masonic Trial Master found that the commissions received by the
accused were not secretly obtained and found the accused not guilty of that
Masonic offense charged .

In Trial Record No. 2296. a former member of Warren G. Harding Lodge
No. 579, was charged with the commission of a lewd and lascivious act upon
a chid, twelve years of age. on or about September 8, 1983 He pied gu~ty
to a violation of Penal Code Section 288a.
Charges of unmasonic conduct were filed on January 13, 1989. He was
found guilty of unmasonic conduct and the penalty was fixed at expulsion.
This was affirmed by Grand Lodge in October, 1989.
He applied for restoration on May 5, 1992 He has offered letters of
personal reference and recommendations. He has further shown remorse
for his actions and apologized to all Masons His former lodge voted 21 to
5 against restoration. While this vote is only advisory, your committee feels
insufficient time has passed since expulsion. It was the consensus of the
committee that a future request for restoration accompanied by necessary
letters of personal reference and recommendations and a leller expressing
remorse for the past actions may be favorably received
We therefore submit the following Resolution
RESOLVED. that the petition for Restoration in Trial Record No.
2296 be denied.
Resolution ADOPTED
Trial Record No. 2337
In Trial Record No. 2337, a member of Hemet Lodge No. 615 was
charged in two specifications with secretly receiving real estate commissions in land transactions.
In the Superior Court of Riverside County, the accused pied guilty to
violation of Government Code Section 1090. a felony. to wit :
Receiving commissions in conflict of interest with the Hemet Valley
Hospital District of which the accused was a Director at the time of the
offense.
The remaining criminal charges were then dismissed pursuant to a plea
bargain and the accused was ordered to make restitution.

395

Your committee. after eX1ensive review and discussion, finds that to
~Ila'."' t~e T.rial Master's findings to stand would constitute a miscarriage of
1ust1ce 1n view of the guilty plea in criminal court.
Based upon the foregoing, your committee submits the following
Aesolulion:
RESOLVED, that the findings of the Trial Master of not guilty be set
aside. new charges be prepared, and a new Masonic Trial be
conducted. consistent with the accused plea of guilty in Su perior
Court, in a lodge other than Hemet lodge No. 615. in accordance
with the California Masonic Code.
Resolution ADOPTED
Trial Record No. 2341
In Trial Record No. 234 1. a member of Yucca Valley Lodge No. 802 was
charged in one specification as follows:

.

. That on or about the 6th day of July, 1983. he did enter into an agreement
with another Mason, now deceased. to invest funds for him with a minimum
yield of 10%. The initial and subsequent investments totaled approximately
$90,000. To date. his son and heir, has been unable 10 obtain a satisfactory
a?c~unting of the invested funds These funds have apparently been totally
d1ss1pated. Several letters from his attorney requesting information have
gone unanswered
A Trial by commission was held After deliberation. the commission
found the accused guilty of the specification and fixed the penalty at
expulsion.
California Masonic Code Section 3312 provides. in part:
''To constitute a Masonic oHense the act of proposed accused must
involve a breach of faith or moral turpitude of the type discussed in
this Code.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

GRANDLODGE OFCALI FOA N~

Business disputes not involving fraud do not constitute Masonic
offenses since brethren may honestly differ as to the terms of an
agreement.

at 5:00 p.m., followed by a formal banquet. The Sunday session starts wilh
breakfast at 7:00 a m.. that includes a devotional service. and the retreat
concludes by noon.

In the instant case, the accused is not charged with fraud. At best . there
exists a business dispute. A Masonic lodge is not the proper tribunal before
which members may seek to enforce payment of debts. or try their business
dispures. Not receiving a satisfactory accounting does not rise to the level
of a Masonic offense.

The Senior Wardens Retreats were held at the Sheraton Concord on
March 6-8. with 94 Wardens and 68 wives in attendance; and at the Holiday
Inn Crowne Plaza. LAX, on March 13-15. with 92 Wardens and 63 wives in
attendance. The retreats were under the direct ion of our Deputy Grand
Master R. Stephen Doan. Each breakout group was given several case
problems to solve and their solutions were presented to all breakout groups
The wives were divided into breakout groups under the direction of the
Deputy Grand Master's wife. Donna, and on the last day of the retreat joined
their husbands group for the final case study.

396

Your Committee on Trial Review has conducted a careful and extensive
review of the entire Trial Record in the matter. Based upon that review, your
committee believes that the specification does not state a Masonic offense
chargeable pursuant to the California Masonic Code and that the finding of
guilty and penalty of expulsion be reversed.
Your committee therefore submits the following Resolution ·
RE SOLYEO, that the findings of the Trial Commission in Trial Record
No. 2341 be reversed, and that a finding of not guilty be entered .
Resolution ADOPTED.

REPORT OF THE COMMITIEE ON
WARDENS MANAGEMENT RETREATS
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F & A.M. of California.

Since the inception of the Retreats there have been many changes. the
first was dividing the Wardens and their wives into breakout groups
geographically located. the size was kept between 10-20; the second was
to develop a notebook divided into approximately 50 sections for the
Wardens to use during the retreat and their term in o ffice: and the third was
to hold the Retreats ir, March and May of each year The word retreat is a
misnomer because the Wardens and their wives spend most of their time in
meetings. The Friday session starts at 7:00 p.m. and concludes at 10:30
p.m. The Saturday session starts with breakfast at 7:00 a.m and concludes

397

The Junior Wardens Retreats were held at the San Ramon Marriott on
May 15-17. with 92 Wardens and 54 wives in attendance; and at the Holiday
Inn Crowne Plaza. LAX, on May 29-31. with 87 Wardens and 54 wives in
attendance. The retreats were under the direction of our Senior Grand
Warden Warren J. Blomseth . Again each breakout group wcis given several
case problems to solve and their solutions were presented to all breakout
groups The wives were divided into breakout groups under the direction
of the Senior Grand Warden's wife, Dee At these retreats. we tried using
overhead projectors for the group presentations and it appears that they
were more usefu(and we will be using them in the future.
The retreats will follow the same scheduling in the future and it is hoped
that the Northern Retreats will continue to be held at the San Ramon Marriott
and the Southern Retreats will continue to be held at the Holiday Inn Crowne
Plaza. LAX.
The assigned speakers were Charles Alexander. Warren Blomseth, Paul
Boyar, John Cooper. Victor Howard, John Rose. William Stovall. and Robert
Winterton. Sr. Each did an exceptional job and all were well received by
those in attendance.
A special thank you to Vic Howard who was instrumental in developing
the case studies: to R. Stephen Doan for the development of the Wardens
notebook: and to the Grand Lodge staff who assisted with the registration
and the attendance roster.

GRAND LODGE OF CALIFORNIA

Also. a very special thank you to the wive s of the commillee members
who assited whenever possible to help make the retreats a success

This year, this committee was charged by Grand Master Stanley M.
Cazneaux with two additional tasks: ones that we whole heartedly accepted.
The first was to endeavor to get the lodges involved with the youth. not an
easy task to do considering the ages of some of our lodges. But we did just
that , the members of this committee went out to the lodges and coordinated
meetings and introduced lodge members to the local youth groups with
much success. The second and largest challenge that he charged us with,
was one of gening two new adult advisors for every member on this
comminee. This was a very large task indeed as several areas in the state
do not have youth groups and others have full boards and need no new
advisors. The comminee is composed of 58 members spread across the six
divisions with each member having approximately 8-10 lodges and 4-10
youth groups assigned to them. Our goal was therefore 116 new advisors.
Grand Master Cazneaux this comminee is extremely pleased to report to
you that we have acquired a total of i l l new advisors thus achieving our
goal plus one.

REPORT OF THE COMMITTE ON
YOUTH ORDERS

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California·

The Masonic Youth Orders have shown unparalleled enthusias~, ~oGd
fellowship and continued cooperation again this year. throughout ~his 1unsdiction. The members of our youth groups have bee~ the ~~1tome of
California on the "R.l.S.E:. As in the past . the lodges with thnvmg you_th
groups continue to be the ones with gains in mem~e~ship and community
involvement There is a correlation betweE;>n the act1v1t1es of our youth and
the growth of our lodges and Eastern Star.
Your comminee on Youth Orders continues to have as its p~ 1 mary
functions two main themes; To develop ettective Fraternal relations with the
members and adult leaders of the three Youth Orders and to promote the
increase of youth programs in the lodges. Additionally we encoura_
ge the
members of our lodges to provide additional adult leadership to their local
youth groups.
As was reported last year, and I am pleased to report this ~ear: the three
Masonic Youth Groups represenled by four distinct orgarnzat1ons have
again had a very small decline in membership. They are doing the thmgs
that are necessary to turn membership around and g~t back on the right
track in moving total membership upwards. This trend will continue upwards
with our help and support. but it will take the dedication and support of _all
of us to keep it going up. We, my brethren, must not get complacent w11h
letting them do it by themselves. We must all do our part to help and support
them in there efforts. There are still Assemblies. Bethels and Chapters
closing. not because of the lack of members. but for the lack of volunteer
adult support. We ask you. the members of this Grand Lodge. to go back
to your districts and seek out those youth groups and find out what you can
do to help them. Masonry remains in the hands of our youth, we grow when
they grow, and we fail when they tail.

399

stituent lodges. other accomplishments were noted.
The Senior DeMolay Leners Program has seen over 800 congratulatory
leners mailed to every DeMolay who achieved his 21st birthday during the
past year.
Our publication of the Masonic Oriented Scholarships continues to be
a most sought after booklet, with weekly requests for more still continuing
to come in.

meeting between the Adult Leaders of the four Yout h Organizations and the
members of the executive comminee was held under the auspices of the
Youth Orders Committee. It continues to serve as an open forum for all
attending and to interact and share mutual areas of concern as well as ideas
for growth and prosperity. It was most pleasing to have all the members of
the executive committee and representatives from the four groups in anendance this year.

Our membership referral program and accompanying article in the June
issue of the California Freemason magazine coincided with the Grand
Masters proclamation of Masonic Youth Month. The program seeks the help
from all 140,000 Master Masons in this jurisdiction to refer at least one
non-Masonic youth relation or acquaintance for membership in one of lhe
three Masonic Youth Orders. In the last three years of this program over 250
referrals have been forwarded to the respeclive adult leaders.

I wish to thank the Grand Master for his confidence in me and the
members of this committee. It has been a pleasure serving with you and the
other members of the execut ive comminee. and been a part of the great
success that you have made of your year. Also a special thanks to the Very
Worshipful John Cooper. Grand Secretary and his statt for the wonderful
cooperation they continue to give. I wish to thank every member of this
Committee with out whom none of this would be possible. They all serve
this Grand Lodge with distinction, dedication and enthusiasm. Lastly. and
most of all, a very large thank you to the members of the Masonic Youth
Orders. They are a joy to work with and for. They are truly the future of our
Order

CORRESPONDENCE REPORT
ANNOUNCEMENT
Review of the Proceedings of other Grand Lodges have appeared
quarterly in the California Freemason, copies of which have been sent to
each Grand Lodge. This program will be continued.
The California Freemason is issued quarterly and each copy carried
additional reviews. Hence. they do not appear in these Proceedings.
